4659 File spark watched-directory mutation probe: raw transcripts (SANITISED)
==============================================================================

What: transcripts of a throwaway standalone probe, NOT the real File mechanism. It opens a
directory the way agents/core/src/spark_file.cpp does (FILE_LIST_DIRECTORY, share
read|write|delete, OPEN_EXISTING, FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS | FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED, IOCP,
ReadDirectoryChangesW with the mechanism's kFilter 0x5b, 32768-byte buffer, bWatchSubtree =
FALSE), reissues the read after a data completion and does not reissue after a failed
completion, then mutates the watched directory. The probe source is not committed.
Summary and interpretation: R5.7 (c) in docs/spark-stage2-guardian-consumer-design.md and #4676.

When and where: 2026-09-21. Windows 11 Pro 25H2 build 26200.9457. Local cases ran on local NTFS.
Probe built with MSVC 14.44.35207 cl.exe (/nologo /EHsc /std:c++17 /W4 /O2 /MT), SDK 10.0.26100.0.

Local cases (one fresh process per run, all runs included, each in full):
  rdcw_rename_probe.exe <case> <work-dir> --run <N> --phase-sec 30 [--close-on-fail | --no-watch]
  case 0  control: create a file in the watched dir; positive comparator (SetFileTime on a
          child); self-touch of the watched dir (SetFileTime and SetFileAttributesW on it)
  case 1  MoveFileExW: rename the watched dir to a sibling name
  case 2  MoveFileExW: move the watched dir under a different parent
  case 3  RemoveDirectoryW on the watched dir
  case 4  MoveFileExW: rename the PARENT of the watched dir
  Each case has two 30 s observation phases: phase 1 the mutation alone, phase 2 probe writes.
  --close-on-fail: after a failed completion, CloseHandle the watch handle (as the
  mechanism's handle.reset() does). Used for the case 3 runs labelled close-on-fail.
  --no-watch: case 4 with NO watch handle opened and 5 s phases (--phase-sec 5). A
  supplementary control showing the parent rename succeeds when nothing holds the directory.

SMB cases (all runs included, each in full; S2 and S3 were single runs):
  rdcw_rename_probe.exe <S0|S1r|S1d|S2|S3> \\<smb-host>\yuzu4659 --run <N> --trigdir <dir>
  Windows client to SMB 3.1.1, signed, not encrypted. Server-side actions were performed by a
  driver on the Linux host; its logs follow each probe transcript.
  S0   control: client-side create, then a server-side create, in the watched dir
  S1r  server-side rename of the watched directory
  S1d  server-side rm -rf of the watched directory
  S2   server hung (docker pause), then unpause, then a server-side create
  S3   server gone (docker stop), then start, then a server-side create; then reopen
  Server caveat: the share was served by Samba 4.23.10 in a Docker container on a Linux host
  over a Tailscale network. It is NOT a Windows file server. S4 (silent packet drop via an
  outbound firewall block) was scoped out and was NOT measured.

Sanitisation: the transcripts are otherwise verbatim (timestamps, PIDs and salted directory
names untouched). Mechanically replaced: the Tailscale address of the SMB host with
<smb-host>; the Windows account and machine names with <user> and <rig> (including the
C:\Users\<user> path segment and the <rig>\<user> SMB account). A sweep for the Linux host and
account names found none in these transcripts. Removed: the ssh post-quantum warning banner
lines, blank lines, trailing whitespace and carriage returns. No credential was written to any
transcript. The share and account name yuzu4659 is a throwaway and is left in place.

Sections below are separated by a line of "#" characters.
==============================================================================
